- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `quantum nonlocality without entanglement`  `state discrimination`  `LOCC`  `minimum-error discrimination`

### TL;DR

The paper demonstrates that quantum nonlocality without entanglement is measure-dependent by constructing product-state ensembles where LOCC achieves optimal unambiguous discrimination but fails for minimum-error discrimination.

### Motivation

To clarify whether the concept of quantum nonlocality without entanglement is robust to the choice of discrimination measure, as this affects foundational understanding and practical protocols.

### Method

The authors construct explicit families of six linearly independent, equally probable bipartite product states and analyze LOCC performance under minimum-error and unambiguous discrimination criteria, extending to multipartite cases with numerical simulations.

### Result

They show a clear separation between the two measures for the constructed ensembles, with LOCC optimal for unambiguous discrimination but not for minimum-error discrimination, and provide numerical evidence for similar behavior in multipartite systems.

### Conclusion

The property of quantum nonlocality without entanglement is not measure-invariant, implying that characterizations of such nonlocality must specify the discrimination task, and it highlights the subtlety of LOCC limitations in state discrimination.

*Keywords: quantum nonlocality without entanglement, state discrimination, LOCC, minimum-error discrimination, unambiguous discrimination*

This paper introduces a partition function ratio framework to estimate logical error rates in stabilizer codes, demonstrating that maximum partition function decoding differs from order probability decoding, with implications for decoding strategies and sample efficiency.

### Motivation

The authors aim to provide a more efficient method for estimating logical error curves in quantum error correcting codes, leveraging the mapping to disordered statistical mechanics models.

### Method

They define a ratio of partition functions corresponding to maximum likelihood decoding and compare it to the order probability, which corresponds to a probabilistic decoding strategy. They analyze the toric code under bitflip noise using the Random Bond Ising Model, considering uniform and non-uniform noise.

### Result

They show that the two decoding strategies differ, and that estimation via decoding probability and order probability is more sample efficient than direct failure counting, especially at low noise. They also discuss the utility of ensembling.

### Conclusion

The framework provides a theoretical basis for understanding decoding performance and offers practical advantages in simulation, with extensions to color codes.

### TL;DR

This paper investigates how disorder affects entanglement entropy in non-Hermitian Hatano-Nelson models with skin effects, finding area-law to log-law transitions and disorder-induced entanglement phase transitions.

### Motivation

Non-Hermitian dynamics with skin effects typically lead to area-law entanglement scaling, but the impact of disorder was unknown. The authors aim to understand how disorder changes this behavior.

### Method

They study the dynamics of free fermions in the Hatano-Nelson model with open boundaries, varying disorder strength and measuring entanglement entropy, subsystem entanglement, connected correlation, and mutual information.

### Result

For small disorder, the area-law becomes logarithmic scaling; for larger disorder, it reenters an area-law regime through an entanglement phase transition. At the critical point, entanglement entropy shows universal algebraic scaling. They also demonstrate absence of conformal invariance in the log-law regime and observe similar transitions with periodic boundaries.

### Conclusion

Disorder induces entanglement phase transitions in non-Hermitian systems with skin effects, enriching the phase diagram and suggesting universal scaling behavior at criticality.

*Keywords: non-Hermitian skin effect, entanglement entropy, disorder-induced phase transition, Hatano-Nelson model, entanglement scaling*

- **Tags**: `simulation`  `strain`  `localization`  `center-of-mass conserving`  `lattice gas`

### TL;DR

This paper shows that local pumping in a center-of-mass conserving diffusive lattice gas leads to self-localized steady states, contrary to intuition that activity spreads.

### Motivation

The authors challenge the expectation that local driving delocalizes activity in diffusive systems, investigating the effect of center-of-mass conservation on steady-state localization.

### Method

They study a 1D lattice gas on a ring with paired hops preserving center of mass, local injection of adjacent particles, and on-site pair removal. They derive exact balance conditions and perform simulations to analyze steady-state properties.

### Result

They prove an exact steady-state balance for total on-site pairs, independent of system size and hopping rate, and show collapse of active core width and amplitude under dimensionless parameters. They also predict overlap of multiple driven cores.

### Conclusion

The results identify a simple mechanism for self-localization in constrained diffusive systems, offering insights into controlling particle distribution via local pumping and conserved motion.

*Keywords: center-of-mass conserving, lattice gas, localization, non-equilibrium steady state, diffusion*

This paper investigates the mechanism of superconductivity in magic-angle graphene by screening Coulomb interactions with metallic layers, revealing that the superconductivity is driven by electronic interactions.

To determine whether superconductivity in magic-angle graphene is driven by electronic correlations or phonons.

### Method

Metallic layers are deposited near magic-angle graphene to screen Coulomb interactions, and the superconducting behavior is measured.

### Result

Screening suppresses superconductivity, indicating that superconductivity arises from electronic interactions.

### Conclusion

Superconductivity in magic-angle graphene is electronic in origin, not phonon-mediated.

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae8df1](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae8df1)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `spectra`  `magnetization`  `polarization`  `magnetism`

### TL;DR

This paper uses Mössbauer spectroscopy to study spin-lattice dynamics and magnetic transitions in hexagonal Lu0.5Sc0.5FeO3, revealing noncollinear magnetic ordering and DM interaction-induced ferroelectricity.

### Motivation

The material is a promising multiferroic, but its spin-lattice coupling and magnetic ground state are debated. The authors aim to resolve these using Mössbauer spectroscopy, which can detect subtle magnetic structures.

### Method

They performed 57Fe Mössbauer spectroscopy combined with synchrotron X-ray diffraction and magnetization measurements over 5-300 K.

### Result

They identified a cascade of spin-lattice dynamics: local FeO5 distortions below 180 K, antiferromagnetic ordering at 165 K, and spin-reorientation at 40 K. The magnetic ground state is noncollinear with Γ1 and Γ2 representations, with weak out-of-plane ferromagnetism.

### Conclusion

DM interactions drive noncollinearity and induce negative c-axis polarization via strong spin-orbit-lattice coupling. The study demonstrates Mössbauer spectroscopy's capability in resolving noncollinear magnetic structures.

*Keywords: Mössbauer spectroscopy, multiferroic, spin-lattice coupling, magnetic transitions, noncollinear magnetism, Dzyaloshinskii–Moriya interaction*

### TL;DR

This paper proposes using spin correlations in entangled quark pairs produced at lepton colliders to probe light-quark electromagnetic dipole couplings, leveraging azimuthal asymmetries in dihadron production.

### Motivation

The Standard Model predicts negligible spin correlations for light quarks, whereas new physics dipole interactions would induce distinct spin-singlet or triplet correlations, offering a clean probe of such couplings.

### Method

The authors derive azimuthal asymmetries in inclusive and back-to-back dihadron production from quark spin correlations, and use published Belle/BaBar data and projected sensitivities to constrain dipole couplings, minimizing dependence on fragmentation functions.

### Result

The analysis shows that these measurements can provide significant constraints on light-quark dipole couplings, with reduced theoretical uncertainties from fragmentation and no contamination from other new physics effects.

### Conclusion

This approach offers a novel and clean collider probe of quark dipole interactions, but it is primarily a high-energy physics phenomenology study rather than a contribution to quantum information or quantum technologies.

*Keywords: quark electromagnetic dipole moments, spin correlations, azimuthal asymmetries, lepton colliders, dihadron production*

## 397. The Paradox Of Steady-state Light Diffusion

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-08-10
- **Authors**: André Liemert, Lorenzo Pattelli, Alwin Kienle, Federico Tommasi And Fabrizio Martelli
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa)
- **Tags**: `scattering`  `light diffusion`  `radiative transfer equation`  `diffusion coefficient`  `absorption`

### TL;DR

The paper resolves a long-standing paradox about the absorption dependence of the diffusion coefficient in light transport, showing that time-domain diffusion is self-consistent, while continuous-wave diffusion cannot be asymptotically equivalent to the radiative transfer equation when absorption is nonzero.

### Motivation

The definition of the diffusion coefficient in light transport has been debated for decades, with absorption-dependent forms conflicting with fundamental scaling properties of the radiative transfer equation. The authors aim to resolve this controversy through an exact consistency test.

### Method

The authors employ an analytic approach, proving in the time domain that an absorption-independent coefficient is necessary and sufficient for exact long-time convergence, and in the continuous-wave regime, they analytically show that no scalar diffusion coefficient can achieve asymptotic equivalence when absorption is nonzero.

### Result

They prove that absorption-independent diffusion is exact in the time-domain limit, but in the steady-state case, matching the dominant attenuation length and residue require different coefficients, causing intrinsic errors that they quantify.

### Conclusion

The time-domain formulation is established as the only self-consistent reference for diffusion in optical transport, calling for re-examination of how diffusion is applied in studies with absorption.

*Keywords: light diffusion, radiative transfer equation, diffusion coefficient, absorption, time-domain*

---

### Motivation

To investigate whether nuclear geometry and deformation affect collective flow in small collision systems, providing new insights into the onset of hydrodynamic behavior.

Analysis of ALICE data for O+O and Ne+Ne collisions at sqrt(s_NN)=5.36 TeV, focusing on the elliptic flow coefficient as a function of centrality.

### Result

A larger elliptic flow is observed in central Ne+Ne collisions compared to O+O, consistent with the expected influence of the prolate (bowling-pin) deformation of neon.

### Conclusion

The measurement provides evidence that nuclear shape drives anisotropic flow in light-ion collisions, highlighting the role of initial geometry in small systems.

### TL;DR

This paper presents the discovery and characterization of a new family of lichen-forming fungi from the genus Tangerine, highlighting their ecological significance in terrestrial ecosystems.

### Motivation

Lichens are poorly understood mutualistic symbioses between fungi and photosynthetic microorganisms, and this study aims to improve understanding of their diversity and evolutionary relationships.

### Method

The authors likely employed genomic or phylogenetic analyses of fungal specimens to identify and classify the new family, integrating morphological and molecular data.

### Result

Identification of a new family of lichen-forming fungi, which may have implications for understanding lichen evolution and ecosystem dynamics.

### Conclusion

This work contributes to the taxonomy and evolutionary biology of lichens, providing a foundation for future studies on their ecological roles and symbiotic mechanisms.

*Keywords: lichen, fungi, symbiosis, phylogenetics, taxonomy*
